S.O. 2101-—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (I) of Section 122 of the Income-tax Act, 1961 41 of 
1961) and in supersession of the previous Notification No. 
6345 (F. No. 261/2/85-10) dated 26-7-1985, No, 6462 (F. No. 
261/2/85-01) dated 16-10-1985 and No- 6775 (F No. 261/6, 
S6-ITF) in this regard the Contral Board of Direct Taxes here¬ 
by directs that the Appellate Assistant Commissioners of the 
ranges specified in column 2 of this Schedule bel 0 w shall per¬ 
form their functions in respect of all person and income assess¬ 
ed to Income-tax in the Income-tax Circles, Wards or District 
specified in the corresponding entry in column 3 thereof ex¬ 
cluding all persons and income assessed to Income-tax over 
which the jurisdiction vests in Commissioner of Income-tax 
(Appeals).

S.O. 2104.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 122 of the Income-tax Act, 1961 (43 of 
1961) and all other powers enabling in that behalf and in par¬ 
tial modification of all previous notifications in this regard 
the Central Board of Direct Taxes hereby directs that the App¬ 
ellate Assistant Commissioner of Income-tax of the Ranges 
specified in column (2) of the Schedule below shall perform 
his functions in respect of the persons and income assessed to 
Income-tax or Super-tax in the Income-tax Circles, Wards 
and Districts specified in the coi responding entry in column 
3 thereof:- -

thereof stands transferred by this Notification from one Range 
to another, appeals arising out of Assessments made in that 
Income-tax Circle. Ward or Districts or pjut thereof and 
pending immediately before the date of this notification be¬ 
fore the Appellate Assistant Commissioner of income-tax 
of the Range from whom that Income-tax office Circle, 
Ward or District or pan thereof is transferred to and dealt 
with by the Appellate Assistant Commissioner of Income- 
tax of the Range to whom the said Circle, Ward or District 
or pari thereof is transferred. 

This notification shall take effect from 1-6-1987,

S.O. 21 !4.—Whereas it appears to (he Central Govern¬ 
ment (hat it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport >of petroleum, oil, natural gas, effluent or any 
mineral from village Vagholi, Tahsil AJibagh, District Rai- 
{Md to tillage Agarsure, Tahsil Alibagh. Distirct Raigad in 
the State of Maharashtra, pipelines should be laid through the 
ai’cnc' of Indian Petrochemicals Corporation Limited. Maha¬ 
rashtra Gas Cracker Canrpfex Division Vile Parle fW), 
Bombay; 

And whereas, it appears to the Central Government that 
for the purpose of laying such pipelines, it is necessary to 
require the right of user In the lands described in the 
schedule annexed hereto ; ' 

Now. therefore in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section fi) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
712 GI|87—4 


Pipe Lines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) Act, 
1962 (?0 of 1962), the Central Government hereby notify 
(heir intention to acquire the right of user in the lands re¬ 
ferred to in the schedule; 


■ Any person intciested in the said lands having any objec¬ 
tion for laynig the pipelines through the said rands may 
prefer an ejection within 21 days from the date of the 
notification, to the Competent Authority, Indian Petrochemicals 
Corporation Limited Maharashtra Gas Cracker Complex Divi¬ 
sion, Nogothnne, Tahsil Roha, District Raigad. 


And every person making such an objection shall state 
specifically whether he wishes he heard in person or by a 
legal practitioner.

state specfflcallv whether he wishes to be heard in person or 
by legal practioner :

MINISTRY OF ENERGY 
(Department of Coal) 

New Delhi, the 3lst July, 1987 

S.O. 2131—.Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that coal is likely to be obtained from the lands men¬ 
tioned in the Schedule hereto annexed; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of section 4 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Ac¬ 
quisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957 ), the Cen¬ 
tral Government hereby gives notice of its Intention to pros¬ 
pect for Cdal therein. 

The plan bearing No. C-l(E) IIf/JR/377-1286 of the area 
covered by this notification can be inspected at the office of 
the Western Coalfields Limited (Revenue Department), Coal 
Estate Civil Lines, Nagpur 440 001 or at the office of the 
Collector, Chandrapur (Maharashtra) or at the office of the 
Coal controller, 1, Council House Street, Culcutta. 

All persons interested in the lands covered by this notifi¬ 
cation shall deliver all maps, charts and other documents 
referred to in sub-section (7) of section 13 of the said Act t 0 
the Revenue Officer, Western Coalfields Limited, Coal Estate, 
Civil Lines, Nagpur-440 001, within'ninety days from the date 
of publication of this notification.

tninths from the date of publication of this noti- 
ficaton in the Official Gazette.

Health) Rules, 1954 after , Duse (iii) the following provi¬ 
sions shall be substituted namely :—

shall be exempted trooi the purview of this clause; Provided

transiMing through an airport situated in the yellow fever 
infected area may be exempted from producing yellow fever

during the period of bis entire stay.”

SIO. 2136.—Whereas the Central Government is of the 
opinion that the ancient monument specified in the Schedule 
annexed hereto is of national importance;

sub-section (1) of section 4 of the Ancient Monuments and

said ancient monument will be taken into consideration by ' 
the Central Government.

1 SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the management of Burragarh 
Colliery of M|s. Bharat Coking Coal Limited was 
jusuhed in not regularising Shri lukku Ahir imme¬ 
diately on completion of six months in the post 
of Mechanical Euler in. 1979 7 if not, to wnat 
relief the workman is entitled 

The case of the workmen is that the concerned workman 
Slfti Jukku Ahir is a permanent employee of BCCL. He 
started working since 2Q-12-19G8 and was designated as 
Looseman. Winle uoing the job of a Looseman t e c. ncern- 
ed wokman learnt the job of Mechanical Fitter during hl» 
ott hours. Wnen ’he ii-*.inagcmeiu knew ihat the cou.eined 
worknran was a good mechanical h'tei, he was tiansfpucd 
on the job of Mechanical Fitter in 1977 and since then the 
concerned workman is regularly per forming the duty of 
Mechanical bitter. But his designation remained us looseman. 
The concerned woikman approached the management several 
times for change of his designation hut inspilc of assurance 
his designation of Looseman was not changed. It is the 
policy decision of the management to regularise a workn on 
if he has regularly worked in the job for continuously six 
months but this policy was not applied in the case of ihe 
concerned workman. Alter great pressure by the concerned 
workman the management regularised tire concerned workman 
on the job of Mechanical Fitter on 19-10-84 after a lapse of 
5 years. He was getting the wages of Cat. IV of the wage 
Board all along and wa s assured higher wages at the time 
of icgularhation. His past services were not considered 
when he was regularised on the job of Mechanical Fitter. 
The matter was taken up by the union with the management 
but the management refused to consider the past services 
of the concerned workman through their office letter d*. 
25/29-12-84. Thereafter the union raised an industrial dispute 
before the ALC(C) on 9-1-85. The management participat¬ 
ed in the conciliation proceeding but the concialiation ended 
in failure and thereafter the present reference was made 
by the Govt, of India for adjudication before this Tribunal. 
Had the concerned workman been regularised in 1979 he 
wyuld have been nbfe to appear before the D.P.C. in the 
year 1982 and 1985, for his promotion but he lost two 
chances due to the illegal acts of the management. It is 
submitted that the action of the management in not regularis¬ 
ing the cncerned workman as Mechanical Fitter from 1979 
was illegal and uniuslified and it deprived him of the two 
chances of promotion. The workmen therefore demanded 
that the concerned workman should be given Cat. V,

The case of the management is that the concerned work¬

mg the job of Cut. 11 and Cat. 111. Tnc concerned work¬

lit, and 'when a mechanical helper is capable of perform¬

Tne concerned workman Was ultimately found suitable to be 
appein cd as mechanical fitter in Cat. IV and he was 
berelore regularised as Mechanical Filler in 1984. The case

the eligibility period of 3 year s flops the date, of his r-gulari- 
sation as Mechanical bitter in 1984. The case of the manage- 
rneut further is that there was no scope for a Looseman 
to learn the job of bitter in his off hours as the job of Fitter 
is not available at the tramming level or at the haulage line 
where a Looseman is employed. The concerned workman was 
shifted to Mechanical cadre in 1979 as his own request and 
the management had not transferred of its own accord on the 
job of Mechanical Fitlor. It veas not correct to '.jy Ih-t ; 
was regularly working as Mechanical Fitter since 1977. A woilr 
man suouid be auie io want me jou propeny owi.ue tie is 
regularised on the job being done by hint. The said period 
of learning the job properly may vary from minimum of 6 
months to one year or more depending upon the capacity 
of skid of a workman. The concerned workman was regu¬ 
larised as Mechanical Fitter as soon as he was suitable for 
the said job. The con,erned workman was properly regu¬

sidering all his performance. He could not have been regu¬ 
larised as Cat. V Fitter directly. The concerned workman 
was not entitled to be regularised as Mechanical' Fiber in 
1979 and as such the claim for promotion to Cat. V has no

concerned workn.nn ig not entitled to any relief. 

The point for consideration is whether the concerned work¬

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the management of Nichitpur 
Colliery of M/s. Bharat Coking Coal Limited, in 
not allowing Shri Deoki Bhuia and Sint, Gangajali 
Karnin, wagon loaders to join their duties was 
justified 7 If not, to what relief are these workman 
entitled 7" 

In this reference both the parties appeared but only the 
workmen fled their W.S.' Thereafter several adjournments 
were granted to the employers. Ultimately on 19-6-87 both 
the parties appeared befoie mo and filed a memorandum of 
settlement. I heard the parties on the said settlement and I 


find that the terms contained therein are fair proper and 
beneficial to both the parties. Accordingly I accept the same 
and pass an Award in terms of the settlement which forms 
part of the Award as Annexure. 

14-7-87.

AWARD 

The Government o'f India, Ministry of Labour in exercise 
of the powers co ifeired in them under Section 10(1 )(d) of 
the I. D. Act., 1947 has retened the following dispute to this 
Tribunal for adjudication vide tnoir Order No. L-i(JW12(27Gj/ 
85/D.IIl(AJ, daied, the 27th February, 198G. 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the management of Kusunda 
Colliery in Kusunda Area No, VI of M/s. Bharat 
Coking Coal Limited in refusing to refer Shri 
Chandradeo Singh, Night Guard, who was super¬ 
annuated from service in inly, 1984 to Medical 
Board for assessment of age as per the JBCCI deci¬ 
sion is justified 7 If not, to what relief the workman 
is entitled 7" 

The case of the workmen is that the concerned workman 
Shri Chandradeo Singh was working as a Night Guard, in 
Kusunda Colliery of M/s, B.CC.L. since 1951. On 24-7-84 
the Supdt. of Mines, Kusunda Colliery issued a nofice to the 
concerned workman stating that he had attained the age of 
60 .years on 31-12-1983 and should have been retired with 
effect from 1-1-84. But as he was not retired on that dale 
he was being retired from the services with immediate effect 
i.e. from 24-7-84. On receipt of the above nolice the con¬ 
cerned workman made a representation dated 2-8-84 to the 
Supdt. of Mines, Kusunda colliery stating that the date of 
his birth recorded in identity card No. 50481 was 1-7-1928 
and as such the date of his superannuation should be 1-7-88 
and requested that he should be allowed to resume his duties. 
The inahagement refused to consider the case of the con¬ 
cerned workman and thereafter an industrial dispute was 
raised by the union vide its reprementation dt. 29'T0-84 to 
the ALC(C), Dhanbad. The management in their comment 
before the ALC(C), Dhanbad contended that the age of the 
concerned workman was recorded as 48 yetirs in Form B 
Register which was prepared after the take over of the 
colliery by the Central Government in October, 1971 and 
that tho management had no record of the age mentioned 
in the C.M,P,F. Form. The management had apparently 
no record of age/date of birth ol the concerned workman 
at the time of his appointment in the colliery in 1951 and 
there was serious discrcpa-cv in the age of the concerned 
workman recorded in Form B Register prepared in 1971 and 
the date of his birth as mentioned in the identity card issued 
to him, and C.M P.F records and as such the union demand¬ 
ed that the concerned workman ‘hould he reinstated with 
full waacs and other benefits from 24-7-84, the date when 
the services of the concerned workman was terminated. 

The management refused to nccede to the demand of the 
union and also refused to accept the suggestion of the 
ALC(C) , Dhanbad to refer Ihe concerned workman 


to the Medical Board for assessment of his age as per JBCCI 
circular. The action of the management of Kusunda Colliery 
in terminating the services of the concerned woikman by 
superannuating him with effect from 24-7-84 is wrong, illegal 
and totally unjustified and that the refusal uf the manage¬ 
ment to refer the concerned workman to the Medical Board 
for the assessment of his age as per JBCCI decision is not 
justified. On the above plea it was submitted on behalf of 
tho concerned workman that ho is entitled to be reinstated 
in, service with full back wages and other benefits with effect 
from 24-7-84 till the date of his reinstatement. 

The case of the management is that the concerned work¬ 
man was rightly superannuated after he attained the age of 
superannuation. The age of the concerned workman was 
recorded as 48 years in Form B Register maintained under 
Section 48 of tho Mines Act, 1952. The said Form B Regis¬ 
ter was written in the year 1971 between the period from 
17-10-71 till the end of 1971 and his year of birth was 
1923 on the basis of the calculation. The concerned workman 
had declared his date of birth as 1-7 1923 at the time of 
recording tho same in the identity card register. There is no 
discrepancy in the age of the concerned workman recorded 
in Form B Register and the identity card register. Had there 
been any glaring error in recording the age in 1 orm B 
Register and Identity card register, approximate age of the 
concerned workman could have leeu obtained on the basis of 
assessment by the medical board. The age recorded In statu¬ 
tory Form B Register and the date of birth recorded in Offi¬ 
cial Identity card Rcgidcr dulv authenticated by Ihe concerned 
workman bind the concerned workman and the union and no 
dispute enn be raised on that issue. The date of birth of the 
concerned workman in the identity card register is 1-7-83 and 
as,such the date of his birth in the identity card cannot be 
1-7-1928 as the date of birth is recorded in Ihe identity card 
on the basis of the dam cf birth noted in the identity card 
register. The date of birth recorded in the identity card 
L7-192R is concoction and fabrication. Form B Reg'sie,- is 
required to be maintained under the Mines Act. 1952 during 
the period of private management of the collieries and the 
aec column of the workmen were required to be filled in 
The management of BrrL prepared its own Form B Rcgisler 
on the basis of the old Form B Register of the private man¬ 
agement and os such it cannot he said that there was no re¬ 
cord of ace of the concerned workman during private tnan- 
ntyment The dam of birth of the concerned workman was 
entered in the TD Card register op the basis of the declara¬ 
tor n 1-7-1923 Them, t 5 serious discrepancy tin the re- 
cord'ne to age ip d’ffermt registers. On the above nlea it 
has been unwed op behalf of the management that the con¬ 
cerned workman is no* entitled to nnv relief 

The only point for decision in this case is whe'hcr it is 
a fit rase in whioh the concerned workman should have 
been referred to the Medical Board for assessment of his age. 

The management and the workman have each examined one 
witness i-> support of their re r psctive case. The workmen have

2. The case of the concerned workman, Bhupnli Bhutan 

Chukraborty, is as follows : 

The concerned wotkman was appointed in the capacity 
of Compounder at Kessurgarh Colliery on 29-10-1972 under 
M‘s. Bharat Coking Coal Ltd, His date of birth was record¬ 
ed as 4-11-1921, but the management retired him from 
set vice on 21-7-1979 before he reached the age of superannua¬ 
tion of 60 years. In the circumstance the concerned work¬ 
man has prajed that the order of the management retiring) 
him from service with effect from 21-7-1979 is not justified 
and that he should be reinstated in service with effect from 
that date and be, allowed to perform his duty till he reaches 
Ihe age of superannuation on 4-11-1981. 

I 

3. The management in tbeir written statement has stated 
that the concerned workman is a literate person and he was 
working as Compounder at Kessurgarh Colliery. His son, 
J. Chakravorty, is an oveimun and his date of ninh is 
1-4-1929, His son is an educated man and he obtained over¬ 
man’s certificate and that he submitted his age before the

of applying as a candidate for examination held in connection 
wi’h selection of overman. Jt is absurd that the date of 
birth of the concerned workman was 4-11-1921 when the 
dale of birth of his son is 1-4-1929. The concerned work¬ 
man failed to cuoitca i.ue hi, ilnim that his date of binh 
was 4-11-1921 and consequently he was sent to a Medical 
Board for determination of his a sc. The Medical Board 
examined him on 1-2-1977 and declared hla age as 58 years 
or more on the date .of examination. He accepted his age 
as 58 years and put his signature on the reports of the 
Medical Board. In the circumstances it is asserted that the 
concerned workman was rightly superannuated with effect 
from> 21-7-1979, the age oTfuperanhuation being 60 years. 

4. The management examined Sri S. K. Singh, Personnel 
Officer of Area-1 of M/s. B.C.C. Ltd. and introduced in evi¬ 
dence a mass of documents which have been marked us 
Ekt9. M-l to M-3. 

5. Admittedly, the concerned workman Is a literate person 
and was appointed in the capacity of a Compounder at 
Kessurgarh colliery now vested in M/s. B.C'.C, Ltd., MW 1 -] 
S. K. Singh has stated that be has been working as Per¬ 
sonnel Officer of Area-I of M/s, B.C.C. Ltd. since 2-5-76 
and that Area-I was split up into two in 1984 as Arca-I 
and Block-IT and that Kessurgarh colliery was placed in 
Biock-11. He has further stated that ns per extent rule nl! 
workmen of collieries under M|s. B.C.C. Ltd. re#ch the age 
of superannuation at the age of 60 years and that the 
concerned workman wax superannuated with effect from

the concerned Workman, as per Identity Card, was 4-11-1921 
while the date of birth of his son J, Chakravorty working in 
Beuedih colliery under the same block was 1-4-1929. He 
has also stated that in view of the age of the concerned 
workman and his son as recorded in the records there arose 
suspicion with regard lo the actual dale of birth of the 
concerned workman and that the matter was referred to 
Medreal Board consisting of Dr. P. N. Pnndey and Dr. B. P. 
Singh.'His testimony further establhhes the fact that both 
the doctors examined the concerned workman and submitted 
medical report separately and a s per the medical report the 
concerned workman reached the age of 58 years on 1-2-1977. 

1 hate persued the medical reports submitted by Dr. 

P, N, Par ley and Dr. B. P. Singh which have been marked 
Fx’s. M-1 and M-2. In bolh the reports the age of the 

concerned workman was determined to be 58 years on 

1-2-1977 The concerned workman has signed the reports 
of the Medical Board and his signatures have been marked 
Exis. M-l /t and M-2/1. It lias been asserted by 

MyM S. K. Singh that the concerned workman acccqtcd the 
report by putting his signatures thereon. In view of thd 
fact that the da’e of birth of the son of the concerned work¬ 
man was recorded as 1-4-1929, the age of the concerned 
workman cannot by any s’retch be 4-11-1921. This is also 
established by the reports of the Medical Board where the

age of the concerned workman was determined to be 58

Inc only point foi determination in this reference it. 
whettiei ihe concerned workman had completed the age 
ul 6U years on 13-4-y5. 

rile management and the wtn kiucn have eacn esummed 
one witness in support of their cate. Ext. M-l and M-2 
pave neen marked on bcnult of the management and Ext. 
VV-1 and W-2 base been marked on behalf of the workmen. 

According to the management the date of bum ot the 
concerned whitman was i5-4-z:> and accordingly they jusu- 
ned the superannuation ot the concerned woikman with effect 
iiom 15-4-1985. Inc case ot the workman, on the other 
hand, is that tns date of birth was 13-4-19zo and as such 
the date ot his, superannuation would bo 15-4-1986 and 
mat he was prcmaturciy superannuated one year prior to the 
aelu.iL date of his supeninnuation. Thus ouly £oint of diffe- 
lencc between the management and the workmen js whether 
the date of birth of the concerned workman was 15-4-1925 
or it was 15-4-1926. 

hst. M-l is an extract Horn Foim B Registei. At M. 
No. 1 5 ot the Ext. M-l it win appeal that the date of birth 
of me concerned woikman Mm u. C. "Iewary is noted o-, 
J.s-4 1925. fho management nad also produced the original 
Mum B Registei and the iribun^ had the opportunity to 
peruse Lhc same. MW-1 in his cross-examination has stated 
that the particulars of a workman on appointment arc tilled 
up m f-oim B Register. f'he said Form tl Register ot which 
Ext. M-f is an extract v v us prepaied by BCCL Qn the basis 
of Foj m B Register of ihe erstwhile management in respect 
of the workmen who were working under the pistwhde 
maiiiigement. He has stated that the Form B Register of the 
ci'stwmle managemem is not available. He has further 
stated that he was not posted m Govindpur Colliery in 
the ycai 1973 when Form B Register was prepared by the 
present management. Fie has also stated that the identity 
card is the only document given to a workman in respect 
of service particulars stated *n Form B Register. It was 
suggested that '15-4' in the age column of lhc concerned 
woikman Ext. M-l u m ditfereni ink from the year '1925' 
written in the said column to which he has denied but when 
J perused the original Form B Register it was apparent that 
the ink ol "13.4'' was different from the ink ot “1925" in 
the age column and the difference is also visibly marked in 
the photo copy Ext. M-l as the writing “15.4” is deep while 
the writing ‘1925’ in faint. This is because of the difference 
in the coloui of the ink used in wilting the said age column. 

Ext. M-2 is the identity card register. It will appear 
from the identity card register ihat in hi. No. 20403 the 
age of the concerned woikman G. C. Tewary is noted as 
15-4-1925.—On careful perusal of the year “25"’ in Ext, M-2 
it will appear that in ‘‘25" the numerical No. “5” has been 
overwritten on "6”. Thus it is clear (hat the year “25" 
m the age column of the concerned woikman has been ovei- 
writing on "26”. This shows that the age of the concerned 
woikman was oiiginally noted as 15-4-1926 in the identity 
card register Ext. M-2 and that an attempt has been made to 
make it 15-4-25 by overwriting so as to make the date of 
birth as 15-4-25. Ext. W-2 is the identity card issued to the 
concerned workman by the management on 30-7-73. The date 
of birth of the concerned workman is clearly written as 
15-4-26 in the said identity card Ext. W-2. MW-1 has 
himself has staled that the particulais in identity card is 
prepared on the basis of the identity card register and the 
identity card register is prepared on ,the basis of Form B 
Register. It appears that as 15-4-26 was the date of birth of 
the concerned workman noted in the identity card register 
in 1973 and as such Ihe date of birth of the concerned work¬ 
man was written ns 15-4-1926 in the identity card Ext. W-2 
which was copied from the identity card register. The correc¬ 
tion in the identity card register Ext. M-2 appears to have 
been made subsequently by overwriting on th<’ year of birth 
"26” and making it “25". T hold that the date of birth as 
given in the identity card Ext. W-2 as 15-4-26 was in accor¬ 
dance with the date of birth iccorded in the identity card 
register Ext. M-2 at the time when the identity card 
was issued in 1973. I have already stated that the date of 
birth of the concerned workman in Form B Register Ext. 
M-l appeals to be doubtful in nature because of the use of 
different ink in the date of birth. Tt has been admitted 
by MW-1 (hat Ext. M-l was prepared on the basis of the 
Farm B Register of the erstwhile management. If the date 
of birth of the concerned workman w r as written as 15-4-1925 


in ihe Form B Register of the erstwhile management there 
was no reason tor ihe management’s stall to write the oale 
oi buih ot the concerned workman only in a diffeicnt Ink. 
In older to establish the fact that the date ol birth was 
earned out in Eju. M-l from Form B Register of the 
ciawhile management, the management should have produced 
lhc r-orm B Register of the erstwhile management and on 
a mere statement of MW-1 that the Form B Register of the 
lime of the erstwhile management is not available is not 
cnougli to show ihe genuineness of ihe dale of birth of the 
concerned workman in txt. M-l which has been made doubt¬ 
ful because of the use of different ink in the date of birth of 
the concerned workman. 

WWrl G. C. Tewaiy is the .onceined woikman. He has 
slated that he was appointed during the erstwhile manage¬ 
ment ot the Uovindpur Colliery as Munshi and that his 
date ot bitth was noted as 15-4-1926 in Form B Register 
of the erstwhile management. He has further stated that in 
identity card his date of birth was stated as 15-4-26 and we 
uo not find- from Ext. W-2 ihat his assertion is correct so 
far the entry ot his date of birth as 15-4-26 is concerned 
in the identity card. He has slated that lie has lelired on 
15-4-85 when he was aged 59 years only. He has stated 
that he does not remember his C.M.P.F. number. The 
management did not produce the declaration in Form A 
which is sent to the Commissioner, C.M.P.F. although the 
copy of the said F’orm is maintained by the office. Even if 
the said Form was not available in ihe office of the manage¬ 
ment the management could .have called for the declaration 
in Form A from the C.M.P.F. office to show that the date of 
tiic concerned workman was noied as 15-4-25 in the declara¬ 
tion made by the concerned workman. WW-1 has denied 
that tiic entry of his age as 15-4-25 in Form B Register is 
corredt and that the said entry was made on the basis of 
1 orrn B Registei of fhe erstwhile management 


On consideiation of the entire evidence on the record 1 
hold that the date of birth of the concerned workman was 
originally noted as 15-4-1926 irt the identity caid register 
Fxt. M-2 and the Identity caul Ext. W-2 was issued to the 
concerned workman on its basis stating the age as 15-4-26. 
I further hold ihat the date of birth as noted m Form B 
Register as Ext, M-l u not at all reliable and as such it 
is not at all vafe to rely on the said entry. Accordingly 
I hold that ihe date of birth of the concerned workman is 
1-4-1926 and he should have been superannuated with effect 
from 15-4-86 when lie attained the age of 60 years which 
i- the age of superannuation. 


Jhe workmen have filed Ext. W-l which is the circular 
dated 19-7-77 relating to the constitution of the medical 
,board for age verification of a workman. It will appear 
from the W.S. of the management itself that as theie was 
the diffeience ot age of about one year according to tiic 
disputing par tie-, there was no need lo get the age of the 
concerned workman ascertain hv the medical board as the 
medical board also cannol be able to give the exact dale 
of birth of a person. As the diffeience in the age being 
asserted by the contesting parties is about a year onlv and 
the medicnl board cannot arrive fit ihe exact date of bitth 
of a person and can give only an approximate age, it is 
not worthwhile to refer the concerned workman now to 
tiie medical board for ascertaining his age, Moreover 
as discussed above it will appear that as there is evidence 
appaient from the management's record that the date oi 
birth of the concerned workman was recorded as 15-4-1926, 
the Tribunal has come to a finding that the date of birth of 
the concerned workman is J 5-4-1926, Thus the necessity of 
referring the concerned workman to the medical board foi 
the deferminaiion of his age is not necessary, 

In the icsuit T hold that the action of the managemen; 
of Govindpur Collicrv of M/s, BCCL in superannuating tb< 
concerned workman Sbri G. C. Tewary, Munshi from service 
in April. 1985 was pot justified and I hold that the eoncernec

15-4-1986 Now that the concerned workman had abend’ 
completed moir limn 60 ve.irs of npe, there i, no nred h

AWARD 

The Government of Indiay Ministry of Labour In exercise 
of powers conferred on them under Section 10(1 )(d) of the 
1. D. Act., 1947 has referred the following disputes to this 
Tribunal for adjudication vide their order No. L-20012(297)/ 
85|D. 111(A), dated the 27th Februaiy, 1986. 

THE SCHEDULE 

“Whether the demand ot Colliery Karamchari Sangh 
that the management of the Moonidih Project of 
M/s. Bharat Coking Coal Limited should give 
piomotion to Shri Rami Subhag Tiwari, Electrician 
to the next higher grade from the date on which he 
was said to have been superseded by. the promo¬ 
tion of other workmen junior to him, is justified ? 
If so, to what relief is the workman concerned en¬ 
titled ?’• 

The case of the workmen is that the concerned workman 
Shri Ram Subhag Tewarl wn« appointed as Msc. Mftzdoor 
in Cat. I in the year 1971 in Moonidih Project. He wav 
promoted to the post of Electrician Helper Cat. IT vide 
order dated 28-8-1978 In 1981 he was promoted to the 
post of Electrician Cat. VI and "his position was SI No. 4 


The management had meritorious candidate like the con¬ 
cerned workman for selection and promotion to the post of 
A<stt. Foreman (Electrical). Hie management was not re¬ 
quired to pick up Shri Anand Napit" from Cat. V to hi] up 
the vacant post of Grade-C. The management has exercised 
the power beyond its jurisdiction. Shri Napit performed the 
work of lower category and his promotion had an adverse 
offer t on the concerned workman. The concerned workman 
has necessary skill so as to enable him to reach the highest 
non executive post in his cadre. The workman has again 
been superseded by the promotion of Shri Subhash Chandra 
Prasad who was promoted to the post of As.stt. Foreman. 
Electrician vide order dated 27/28-1-1986 in utter disregard 
to the genuine claim of the concerned workman. 

The management has the promotion scheme in "respect of 
all the employees working in the FJectrical and Mechanical 
cadre. The D.P.C. cannot challange the electrical and 
rocehnicaJ certificate of competency awarded to the concern¬ 
ed workman by the Government of Bihar. The concerned 
workman had again gone to attend the D.P.C. on 7-11-1984 
For the second time but the D.P.C. w r as not held on the 
"aid date ami as such the concerned workman had to return 
back As no interview was held by the D.P.C. for the second 
time, the question of filling the vacant poet of Asstj. Elec¬ 
trical Foreman did not arise The D.P C. had adopted the 
attitude of favotirtism in favour of certain workmen of 
lower erade by riving them double promotion and adopted 
biased attitude against the concerned workman Shri 
Subbash Chandm Pd. was promo'ed with back date without 
holding nnw D.P.C. It has been nraved that the concerned 
workman should he promoted to the post of Asstt. Foreman, 
Flectrical. Grade-C. from Mav. 1985. when he was supersed¬ 
ed bv promotion of 2 other workmen junior to him vide 
order d ited 7/8-5-1985 along with consequential benefit. 

The case of the management is that promotion is the 
management’s function and no workman has any right to 
claim promotion. The management has a promotion schemb 
in respect of D’ the employees working is Electrical 
imd Mechanical Cadre and tlie promotion are affected ac¬ 
cording lo the norms laid doWn in the said scheme. The 
promotion of Electrician are effected on the basis of Wire- 
man’s certificate, performance in trade lest conducted hv 
ihe D.P.C (Departmental promotion committee} and the 
mimhc- of years of experience. The selection arc made in 
order of merit bv nnplvinfi the marks obtained in nualifien- 
1 1 m; experience and unde teu. The case of all eltrihU candi¬ 
date; ire considered bv the D.P C tfter an Electrician

sors hip certificate. An Electrician in Cat. Vi being senior- 
most cannot be promoted to the supervisory post without 
the supervisorship certificate and he has to work under his 
junior,’ A D.P.C. was constituted fqr consideration for pro- 
■ motion of Electricians holding the Electrical Supervisorshlp 
certificate to the post of Asstt. Foreman held its sitting on 
16-4-1985. Theie arc four Electricians including the concern¬ 
ed workman eligible for consideration for promotion. Out

promotion to the post of Asstt. Foreman Electrical as he had 
second 20 marks out of 30 marks in Technical trade test. 
Other Electrician Shri Anandi Napit secured 15 marks out of

marks. The minimum pass marks' in Technical examination 
is 50 per cent and unless a workman secures more than 50 
per cent marks in Technical Trade test he cannot be promo¬

6 months on the then existing category (Cat. V) and was

and his another co-worker Shri Subhash Ch. Pd., got 8

mination. Therefore their case were not recommended for 
promotion. 

Another D.P.C. was constituted after 6 months from the 
first D.P.C, for taking trade test of two electrician eligible 
for consideration. The only eligible candidate Shri Subhash 
Chandra Pd. appeared before the Second D.P.C. and secured 
more than 50 per cent marks in the trade test and was pro¬ 
moted. The concerned workman did not aopcar before the 
scond D.PC. which was held on 29-11-1985 and ns such 
his suitability could not be judged by the D.P.C. The de¬ 
mand of the union for promoting the concerned workman to 
the post of Asstt, Foreman even when he failed in the exa¬ 
mination and test before the D.P.C. on the ground that he 
is senior lo other Electricians who were promoted by the 
D.P.C. is without any merit and is liable to be rejected. 
All the persons who had passed the trade test were nromot- 
ed. Tf the concerned workman had passed the Technical 
trade test he would have also been promoted as vaennev 
existed. The concerned workman was not promoted as he 
failed in the examination conducted b>- the D.P.C. the selec¬ 
tion before the D.P.C. were made according to the 
norms and Technical Trade Test The concerned 
workman did nol nnpear brfore the second D .P. C. 
be felt that he had not good knowledge on the electrical

and. he should he rmtrioted aWomafienllv according to 'he 
seniority. On the above plea it has been submitted nn behalf

tled to anv relief 

The nrdv roim for com idrratlon in this case is whethe- 
the concerned workman Shri Ram Subhnn Tew-we Electrician 
Cat. VT should be .given nromotion to the next higher grade 
of an Asstt, Foreman (Electrical! in Grade-C from the date

junior to him. 

The workmen examined three witnesses and the manage¬ 
ment examined one witness in support of their respective 
cases. 

The documents of the workmen have been marled iv Ex' 
W-l to W-10 and the documents on. behalf of the manage¬ 
ment have been marked Ext. M-l to M-?. Some of the 
documents marked are common to both. 

1 

On perusal of the case of the workmen in the W S, it 
wil appear that they are claiming the promotion of the con 
temed workman to the post of Asstt. Foreman Electrical in 
Grade-C on the basis of his seniority. It appears no doubt, ’• 


that the const mod workman was senior in the list, Ext. W-2, 
to Sarju Ray in the same list by which they were promoted 
to the post of Electrical Helper in Cat, U. Ext, W-3 dated 
9jlU-6-1981 also showi that the concerned woikman was 
senior to Sarju Roy m the list of promotion from Cut. V 
to- Cat, VI. The othor two electricians who had appealed 
before the first D.PC. wete Aymnd Napit and Shri Sub bash 
Ch. who were admittedly Electricians in Cat V and thus 
they wer juinor in grade to the concerned workman. 

The case of the management is mat the pibtuouon in 
Supervisory Guide of Asstt. Foreman and Foreman Incharge 
is made on meiit-cum-senioiity basis and that the promotion 
is made from Electrician Cat. IV, V and VI by the D.P.C. 
after holding trade test. MW-l Shri D. B. Singh who were 
Dy. P.M. in Moonidih Pioject was one of the members of 
the D.P.C. in respect of promotion from Electricians to 
Asstt. Foreman. He has stated that the certificate of Elec¬ 
trical Supervisor is necesary before an Electrician can he 
considered lor pomotlon to the post of Asstt. Foreman 
(Electrical). There is no denial of the fact that the concerned 
workman possesses the eertifiepte of Electrical Supervisor. 
MW-l has further stated that the post of Asstt. Foreman 
and Foreman Incharge are all supervisory post who haye to 
act under Electricity Act and Rules. He has stated that the 
concerned workman along with three others hud appeared 
in the interview before the D.P.C. which was held on 16-4-85 
and thi s fact is admitted by the concerned workman MW-l 
also. Ext. M-l is the tecommendation of the D.P.C. which 
was held on 16-4-1985. It will show that out of 30 marks 
Sarju Roy obtained 20 marks, Anirnd Napit obtained 15 
maiks and subhash Chandra anj the concerned workman 
Ram Subhag Tewary each obtained 8 marks. MW-l has 
stated that alleast 30 per cent mark was required to pass 
the trade Lest for consideration for promotion to the post 
of Asstt. Foreman and as such Shri Sarju Roy who had 
secured more than 50 per cent marks were selected and 
Ananj Napit who had obtained just 50 per cent marks was 
first taken as trainee and thereafter he was taken as Asstt. 
Foreman. It will appear from bis evidence that as the con¬ 
cerned workman and Shri Subhash Ch. Pd. had secured less 
than 30 maiks were not selected. It is clear therefore that 
the concerned workman did not secure the minimum marks 
required for being selected for the post of AbsU Foi eipan 
Electrical. WW-1 in his cross-examination has stated that 
a p.-rson should possesp the Electrical Supervisorship certifi¬ 
cate for promotion to the post of Asstt. Foreman. He has 
further stated that any Electrician having obtained Electrical 
Supervisorship certificate can he called for interview by the 
D.P.C. for the post of Asstt. Foreman. Tf is clear therefore 
from this evidence of the concerned workman that all the 
four electricians who had appeared before the First D.P.C. 
were eligible for appearing for being considered for promo¬ 
tion to the post of Asstt. Foreman. Fxt. M-I shows the 
technical qualification of all the four electricians whp had 
appeared before the D.PC. All the four had electrical su¬ 
pervisorship certificate and as such all the four electricians 
of Cat. V and Cat. V( were eligible for being considered for 
promotion to the post o f Asstt. Foreman (Electrical!. The 
concerned workman can have no grievance that Electricians 
of Cat, V who wire juniors to him were, considered' bv the 
D.P.C. for the promotion to the post of Asstt. Forcmm. 

There is nothing to show that (he members of the D.PC 
had any prejudice against the concerned workman or that 
th<-v had anv reason not In promote the concerned workman 
had he secured more than 50 net cent marks in the trade test. 

Admittedly, a second D.PC. was constituted for holding 
the trade test and examination of the concerned 
workman and Subhas Chandra Pd for considering (heir pro¬ 
motion to the post of A'stf. Foreman Ext. M-4 is the note 

sheet dt. 30/31-12-85 which shows that at first the second 

DP.C, was lo be held on ’7-11-85 but due to ’he absence 
of the Dy. DE. fO&M) Moomdih Area on 27-11-85 the

wwkman did not appear before the D.P.C. on 29-11-85 Ebe 
concerned workman has laid evidence of WW-? and WW-1 

to show that the D.P.C. wn- not held on 27-11-85 at 5P.M 

'’hich was the date fixed for holding ihe D?f TV °Vrt 
fact Is admitted V the r'lvrmtst a' "'ell ">•’■1 the D P C. 
was not held on 27-11-85 rr.J was adjourned to 29-11-85

when the concerned workman did not appear before the

trade test that and accordingly fie was selected for the post of

WW-1 and his associates WW-2 and WW-3 have all stated 
that they had gone together as WW-1 was to appear for the

D.P.C. was not held on 27-11-85 and that the concerned 
workman was told that he will be informed of the date 
subsequently when the D.P.C. will be held. MW-1 who was 
one of the member of the D.P.C. has stated that the con¬ 
cerned workman and Subhash Chandra Pd. were both in¬ 
formed veihally and in writing that the D.P.C. would not 
be held on 27-11-85 and that the same wotild be held on 
29-11-85. In cross-examination MW-1 has stated that the 
letter fixing the date of D.P.C. on 29-11-85 has not been 
filed in this case. He has denied that no information was 
given to the candidate for the interview which was fixed 
for 29-11-85. He has stated that Subhash Chandra and the 
concerned workman had both appeared on 27-11-85 at about 
4 P.M, for interview and that the concerned workman 
verbally told him that he would not appear before the D P.C. 
because of the pendency of the case before the Tribunal 
but the concerned workman did not file any written petition 
to that effect. It will appear from Fxt. M-4 that Subhash 
Ch. Pd. had appeared before the D.P.C. on 29-11-85 and 
his Jrade test was taken, It is admitted by WW-1. that 
Subhash Ch. Pd. had also gone in interview on 27-11-85. 
The fact that Subhash Chandra Pd. had appeared before 
the D.P-C. on 29-11-85 shows that he was informed of the 
-date of the adjourned date when the D.P C. w as to be held 
and I find no reason ns to why the concerned workman 
would not have been informed regarding the said adjourned 
date. I am of the opinion that (he concerned workman also, 
in the circumstances of the case, must have been informed 
on 27-11-85 about the adjourned date when the D.P.C. was 
to be held on 29-11-85. 


the D.P.C., ie not justfflert and accordingly the concerned 
workman is not entitled to any relief. 

This is- my Award.

The concerned workman has made wild allegations against 
Shri D. B. Singh (MW-1) knowing fully well that he was 
the member of the D.P.C. who was to depose in the case on 
behalf of the manageme'T. WW-1 has stated that Shri D. B 
Singh was the Personnel Officer and a member of the Second 
D.P.C. who had detna ded Rs. 500 from him for giving 
him promotion and as he did not fulfil the same he was not 
selected for promotion to ihe post of Asstt, Foreman. WW-1 
has stated that he knows that there is g CBJ depat fment 
where a workman can report icgaiding the demand of ille¬ 
gal gratification but the concerned workman did not make 
any reprirt with the CPI against Shri D. B. Singh. He has 

also stated that he did not make any complaint against 

Shri D, B. Singh for the demand of money made by him. 
It is easy to make such allegation but it is really an uphill 
takR to establish the allegation mare so, when It is com¬ 
pletely bereft of any reality. In my opinion the concerned 
workman capnot succeed by marking such wild alienation 
having no foundation. The concerned workman did not 
appear before the second D.P.C. held on 29-11-85 and as 

such his case could not be considered for promotion to the 

post of Asstt. Foreman although there was a vacancy for 
it. Accprding to the cadre rule the concerned workman has 
to appear before the D.P.C. for ronsirratinn' of his case for 
the prist Of Asstt. Foreman and unless he so appears he 
cannot get nmmorion tbronch back door method on the 
ground of his seniority which alone is not the criteria for 
promotion to the supervisory grade. The promotion to the 
supervisory grade is made on the basis of merit-cum-semoi itV 
and the merit has to be nidged bv the D P.C arfti not bv 
this Tribunal. 


hi the result, I hold that the demand of the Colliery 
Karamchari Snngh that the management of Moonidih Project 
of Mis. B C.C should give promotion to Ihe concerned 
workman Shri Ram Subhap Tewarv, Electrician Cat. VI to 
the next higher grade of Asstt. Forerun in Grade-C from 
May, 1985 the date on which lie is said to have been surer- 
ceded by the promotion of other workmen junior to him bv

AWARD 

The Central Government in the Ministry of Labour vide ils 
notification No. L-12012/269/80-D II A dated 10th July, 
1981 has refenc'd the following industrial dispute to this 
Tribunal for adjudication :— 

"Whether tho action of the management of Cili Bank. 
N.A., New Delhi in posting S/Shri Ravi Kumar 
Gupta, Athok Bhasin, Mrs. Sunila Bawa and Mrs. 
Madhu Sehgal as Sfenograpbei s ignoring the claims 
o, oilier eligible suitable candidates is justified ? If 
not, to what relief are the workmen concerned 
entitled 7” 

2. The case of the workmen as set forth in the statement 
of claim is that the Citi Bank has three different categories 
of posts namely Typist, Clcrk-cum Typist and Stenographers 
dnd all the three posts have different eligibility co ditions/ 
qualifications but these qualifications arc co-relatcd with each 
other as tvping speed efficiency in English and graduate in 
education are the essential Summon features in respect of 
all these posts. In the case of stenographers the additional 
qualification required is competency in shorthand with speed 
of 100 W.P.M. The perso-s working in the category of 
clerk-cum-typist can corape'e for the post of stenographer 
according to recruitment rules if they fulfil the requisite 
qualifications in shorthand. It was agreed between the Man¬ 
agement and the Union that v/he-cver there is vacancy the 
Management shall notify the vacancy on the staff notice 
board? However, the Management ignoring the criteria laid 
down for .Ihe post of stenographer end live mandatory con¬ 
dition of displaying the vacancy on the staff notice-board, 
stealthily appoi"ted" four persons namely Ravi Kumar Gupta, 
Athok Bhasin. Mis Sunita Bawa and Trio Madhu Sehgal ns 
stenographers by ignoring and depriving the legal rights of 
the other'eligible and senior candidates. It is alleged th *t 
th-rr anpoinmrn-fs were mt.de with ulterior moDv.es and 
mala fide intention bv the Management to please their favou¬ 
rites, although neither They were nunlified nor experienced 
in the post of gtenrwnphcrs. This ifi-md action h^d deprived 
the legal rights of <h: most comne'em and eligible en-d dates 
and hence is wholly illegal and unconstitutional and liable to 
be quashed.

2. Thereupon the I Party has filed the claim statement 
and it reads us follows : 

“Smt If N. VicUnwalhi joined the H Party Bank a 8 a 
Clerk on 18-10-72 and she was working at the 
Enuikplam Branch' when her services were termina¬

rary and illegal and against the principles of natural 
jus'ice. The persons given for termination arc not 
genuine and bonafide. They are C'ven to lend 
~olour to their case. Their action amounts to re*j

8-4-82 Is the letter of the management r-fer to Fxt M-1J. 
T t states that *he was given one more chance to loin her d"fv 
on or before 20-4-80. fndbn which the matter would he 
viewed serlouslv, Fxt. M-17 dated 5 6-82 is p letter bv the

that she was not feeling well and she wanted time till 10 8-82 


to join at Ernakulum. She promised she will follow her

management has made it very clear by that letter that though 
she has promised to join on 1-4-1982, she did not do so and 
it shows that probably she had not recovered from her ill 
health and that jf she fails to report on or before 15-6-1982, 
the management will have no alternative than to terminate 
her services as per para-522(l) of Shastry award, The said 
para of Shastri award has been reproduced in Ext. W-4. The 
correspondence thus show* that after she produced two certi¬ 
ficates as per Ext. M-12 and M-13 alongwith her letter Ext. 
M-ll. The management did not entertain any idea of taking 
disciplinary action against her on the ground that her absence 
amounted to misconduct. Ext, W-3 d f . 8-4-82 reflects that 
the management took into account her assurance to join duty 
on 31-3-1982, but since that dale had passed off, she was 
permitted to join on or before 20-4-1982. Though the memo 
states that if she did not join the metter would be viewed 
seriously. It does not mean 'that the management was still 
contemplating disciplinary action against her. The memo dt, 
5-6-1982 Ext, W-4 indicates that even though the manage¬ 
ment had permitted to join on or before 20-4-1982 it had still 
then waited upto 5-6-I9R2. Since there was response from 
the employee it was constrained to issue a memo as per Ext. 
W-4. Ext. W-8 *is the same as Ext. M-17, Fxt. W-9 dated 
15-6-1982 is a letter by the employee and therein she has 
contended that her telegram dated 10-5 82 and letter dated 
5-6-82 may be takq/i into account, She has specially admitted 
if after having receiver) the memo dated 5-6-1982, Ext. W-4 
the management has taken objection for the production of 
the photostat copies of true copies of Medical certificate* 
marked as Ext. W-10 and W-Il. I find tfiat photo copies of 
copies cannot be admitted in evidence, though they are marked. 
They are of no consequency. Even otherwise they are relied 
upon by the workmen to show that still she was under medi¬ 
cal treatment, till 7-2-82. It is on the ground of continued 
ill health on the part of the workman that the management 
intend to terminate her service as Is obvious from the counter 
statement. The letter of the employee Ext. W-9 acknowledlng 
the receipt of Ext. W-4 establish for the management that It 
•was found reasonable to put the employee on guard. That 
it no longer intends to take disciplinary action but intends 
to resort to termination, simpliciter under Clause 522(1) of 
Shastry award. Nothing prevented the employee fiom joining to 
duties on or before 15-6-192 and work. Even.for a single day 
if her absence was not on the ground of continuous ill health. 

I am therefore of the view that it is not a case of termination 
of service on arbitrary grounds or In violation of principles of 
natural justice or in exercise of power in an unbecoming 
manner. For the I party-no justification hns been pointed out 
that the management was under an obligation to wait till 
10-8-82, merely because she had made a request that she may 
be permitted to join at Ernakulam on 10-8-82. The manage¬ 
ment had no obligation to wait as per her letter Ext. W-9 
dated 15-6-1982, which makes special reference about the 
reciept of Ext, W-4 by her. Ext. W-5 dated 12-7-82 In 
another memo it is under this memo that her services were 
terminated on the ground Of her continued Ill health. Ext, 
W-5 was enclosed with a demand draft of Rs. T,631-3RP. 
being the pay and allowances in lieu of 3 months notice, 
Ext. W-6 dated 26-7-82 is a letter and therein she states a* 
follows: 

“...This ha* upset me much especially since I 

have almost completely recovered from my pro¬ 
longed sickness and I assured you that I would possi¬ 
bly report for duty on or before 10-8-82”. 

Ext. W-6 thus points out an admission that she had pro¬ 
longed sickness and It is the very ground on which the 
management has terminated her services. Ext, W-7 dated 
31-7-82 is a reply by the management to the letter Ext. W-6, 
She had been informed that the management is not inclined 
to review its order. The evidence of the employee in para-4, 
the documents at Exts. W-12, W-13 and W-14 relate to es¬ 
pousal and conciliation proceedings. There Is the dispute on 
that point. The mere fact that she has not accepted the draft 
nor encashed the same dogs not show that the action of the 
management contravene clause 522(1) of Shastry Award, , ’

The learned counsel for ihe I party contended that even 
otherwise termination of the services of the employee amounts 
to retrenchment and since the provisions of Section 25 F 
have not been followed the termination is invalid. He 
referred the case of 1976 I LD S.C. in State Bank of India 
vs. Suhderamoney P. 478. 'Ihe authority states that the ter¬ 
mination of services of an employee for reasons what so ever 
amounts to retrenchment und that it is not the form of the 
order, but the substance which is decisive. In the reported 
case on facts it has been held that the termination ant on its 
to retrenchment. "The learned counsel for the It Party con¬ 
tended that the facts of present case differ that it is a case 
of termination for continuous illness and that the definition of 
the word retrenchment does not include termination on the 
ground of continuous ill health as per Sec. 2(oo) (c) of the 
Industrial Disputes Act. The evidence on record discloses that 
before the management issued a memo dt. 8-3-1982, ns per 
Ext', W-3 it had already arrived at a conclusion that she 
should be permitted to join her duties on oi before 24 4-1982 
and if she did not join' her duties on or before 20-4-1982 
they will take up necessary action. What was the intended 
necessary action was made clear by the management by the 
memo. dt. 5-6-1982, Ext. W-4. The employee had by her own 
volition ignored Ext. W-4 and has not care to take any step 
or report to duty till her services were terminated after about 
1 month and 6 days at, per Ext, W-5, Dt. 12-7-1982. 1 am 
therefore of the view that it is not the case of retrenchment 
but termination of services on account of continuous ill 
health. 


9. The learned counsel lor the I Party placed- reliance on 
the authority of Gujarat Steels Tubes Ltd., vs. Gujarat Steel 
Tubes Mazdoor Saba 1980 Lab O.C.P. 1004. The authority 
is on the point that the Court should find out whether the 
termination of services by discharge simplicitor is by way of 
punishment. In my opinion, the termination of service can 
neither he said to be matalide nor in colourable exercise of 
powers by the management. The memo dt. 5-6-1984 Ext. 
W-4 points out to (he tact that the management had made it 
very clear (hat it was going to take action under clause 
522(1) of Sbastry award and it intended to terminate her 
services on the ground of her continued ill health. The fact 
that the management intend not to retrench her but intended 
to terminate her services on the ground of ill health 
hhs been sufficiently implied in the memo Fxt. W-4. 
The employee has not availed of the opportunity given to her 
either by joining or by pleading that her absence was not on 
account of continued ill health. By her various letters enclosed 
with medical certificates and her over declaration, she in¬ 
tentionally caused and made the management to believe that 
she was continuously suffering from 111 heath and further 
mad^the management to grant leave to a considerable length 
of time believing that she was really suffering from ill health 
and now when her services have been terminated on the 
same ground on making her alert that the management in¬ 
tended to have resort to tnke silch an action, she cannot 
plead that her absence was not on the ground of continuous 
111 health. Verily Section 115 of the Indian Evidence Act 
steps in and estops her from doing so. In view of the memo 
Ext. W-4, the action of the management cannot be challenged 
as pumitive. Since it has been held on facts that the action is 
pumltive the authority does not help her. 

10. The learned counsel for the T Party then ci'ed the ca r e 
of M. N- Kufitar vs. May and Baker IP) I_*d.. 11986 Lab. I. 
C.P. 1005). The authority is on the. point that Section 11-A 
of the Industrial Disputes Act (fives additional power to the 
Tribunal and it has to deal with the situation. The vibolry 
was also relied noon to show that the ease involves not 
termniation simplicitor. but to hide'the fact that the ffisrhprtm 
was by way of punishment, it has been already held that it 
is not a case of punishment Pud the management has not 
resorted to colourable exercise of powers. 

' 11. In renly to ihe contention o f the T Parly, that the 
punishment is disproportionate to the nErg'-d act of miscon¬ 
duct, and that the aforesaid authority should be relied upon 
to set aside the ordef of termination. The learned counsel

for the management has referred to the cose of M/s. Marikar 
(Motors) Ltd., vs. State of Karnataka and other (1986 Lab. 

I, C. 1213). The facts ot the reported case me similar and 
that the provision of Section li-A can .ot be i.tvokcd, because 
it cannot be said that Uie termination of set vices wvas not 
justified. Whether the tacts and ciicumstance of the case 
aie such that she de erved some more indulgence at the 
hands of the management especially when the was ready 
and prepared to j'oin the service i by Hh of Augist, 1982 is a 
question which is not lelevant to the points of dispute in view 
of the case 1986 Lab l.C. 1213. 

, I1A. Ihe learned counsel for the I Party contended that 
claue 522(1) of the Miaatry avvaid is not funding on < ihe 
l Laity. it was further contended that such a piovision is 
void under Section 23 oi the Contract Act. He cited the 
ca e of Central .Inland Water Tanspoxt Coipotalion vs. 
Biogon.ith Gangooli (1986 Lab. L.C.P. 1312), The facts of 
the reported ca„e dsciose that the Rules of tuc Corpoi a' ion 
empowered to terminate the services of permanent wotkmen 
without giving any reason and by giving a notice Hnd under 
such ciicumstance:), it has been heir! ilia, the Rule is void. 
Ciau c 522(1) oi Shurtry Award, tu niy opinion cannot be 
challenged in the context of the facts of piesent case and the- 
principles lad down in the authority arc not attracted. It 
had not bten shown that the award is a result haf any con¬ 
tract between the partes or a unilateral Act on the rail 
of the management. 

12. The learned counsel for the I Party then brought to 
my notice the case of Bishwajit Deo Rai vs. Indian Overseas 
Bank (1986 I L1J P. 288). The authority is on the point 
that even in cases where Ihe employee has m> defence at rdl, 
still thou opportunity of being heard, should be given and 
failure to give such opponun ty world v'olate the principles 
of natural justice On facts at hand, it lvis been held that 
EM W-4 fialed 5ih June, 1982 gave her ample opportunity 
to ion to her services if she was npt continuously ill and in 
my view it is not a case where the management was hound 
to issue a charge sheet and hold an rnuniry and then dismiss 
her. The management has proceeded to accent her own rase 
of continued ill health and cautioned her that it was going 
to press into service her own ground on which she had re¬ 
mained absent continuously for such n long reriod. 

13. I do not find (hap there should be any order regarding 
cos's. 

14. In the result an awa-d is pawed to the effect that the 
management was iustifled in terminating her services and 
th't.she is not entitled to any refief.

S.O. 2163.-— In pimuunee of Sub-Section (4) of Section 
3 of tlwAfica Mines Labour YVelfare Fund Act, 1946 (22 
of 19461, the Central Government hereby publish the fol¬ 
lowing report of the activities financed from the Mica Mines 
Labour Welfare Fund during the year ending 31st March, 
1986 together with a statement of accounts for the year 
and an estimates of receipts and expenditure of the said 
fund for the year 1986-87. 

PART-I 


1. General 

The Mica Mines Labour Welfare Fund has been consti¬ 
tuted under the Mica Mines Labour Welfare Fund. Act, 
1946 (22 of 1946) for financing the schemes relating to the 
welfate of Labour employed in the mica mining industry. 

2. The Act providea for the levy of ft duty of custom a» 
on till mica exported upto a maximum rate not exceeding 
6-1/4 per cent ad valorem as may from time to time be 
fixed by the Central Government in the official Gazette. 
However rate of cess was enhanced to 3-1/2 per cent with 
effect from 15th July, 1974, from the firevious level of 2-1/2 
per cent nd valorem. 

PART-II 


2. Medical 

Various types of medical facilities for mica workers and 
their dependents are provided free of cost by the Mica Mines 
Labour Welfare Organisation. These include provision and 
maintenance of hospitals, maternity and child welfare centres, 
facilities for treatment of T B inchnleing domiciliary treatment, 
dispensary service including Ayurvedic dispensaries and other 
facilities etc. The following Central and Regional hospitals 
continued to be maintained by the Welfare Organisation 
for the treatment of the mica miners and their dependents 
during the year under report : -

The Welfare Otganisation has been endeavouring to provide 


adequate facilities for tieatment of the miner* suffering from 
TB. Apart from setting up of the TB Hospitals and clinics, 
4 beds remained teserved in Nellore for providing specialised 
treatment to mica miners of Andhra Piadcsh suffering from 
TB and Sillicosis. Besides, there i» a 10 bedded segregated 
ward in tbc Central Hospital, Gangapur for the purpose. 

Miscellaneous Medical Facilities 


Under the Fatal Accident and Benefit Scheme, the fund 
continued to- provide financial assistance to the spouse of a 
miner in the form of a lump sum payment of Rs. 250/- 
(since levised to RS. 800/-) and monthly allowance of Rs 
75/- payable for a petiod of five years and monthly scholar¬ 
ship of Rs 15/- payable in respect of each school going child 
till the age ofc 15 or is married, whichever is earlier. During 
the period under report an amount of Rs. 1000/- in Bhilwara 
Region and a sum of Rs. 2600/- in Hydetabad region in res¬ 
pect of 2 and 9 workers respectively wctc sanctioned under the 
Scheme. 


Arrangements continued for the treatment of mica miners 
of Bihar suffering from leprosy at the Tetulmary Leprosy 
Hospital or the treatment of mica miners suffering from cancer, 
arrangement continued at the Central Hospital Kalla (Asansol) 
and fo r mental diseases at the Mental Hospital Kanke (Ranchi! 

Four Tubnctomy, 47 Leproscopic operations were conducted 
and 6959 nirodh w'ere distributed during the period under 
repoit. i 

A suhsistance Allowance of Rs. 100/- P.M. is granted to 
a TB patient for a period upto 9 months when he happens 
to he the onlv earning member of the family. 

Educational and Recreational Facilities 

For providing educational and recreational facilities to mica 
workers and their dependents, various multipurpose institutes, 
each compromising of nil Adult Education Centre and Woman 
Welfaie Centre have been opened bv the Welfare Organi¬ 
sation. Eor recreation purposes Radio Sets have been installed 
in the mioamining areas and recreation clubs as well as library 
and reading rooms have been functioning under the Mica 
Mines Labour Welfare Organisation. The number of institu¬ 
tions providing these facilities ate ns detailed below:—

S.O. 2164.—In pursuance of Section 10 of the Limestone 
and Dolomite Mines Labour W«Lfav*..Tund Act, 1972 (62 of 
1972J, the Central Government hereby publishes tho follow-, 
mg report giving an account of its activities financed under 
the said Act during the year 1985-86 together with the state¬ 
ment of accounts for that, year :— 

General 

The Limostoae and Dolomite Mines Labour Welfare fund 
was constituted under the Limestone and Dolomite Mines 
Labour Welfare Fund Act, 1972 (62 of 1972) which provides 
for the levy and collection of cess at a rate not exceeding 
one rupee per metric tonne op so much, of Limestone and 
Dolomite produced in any mine :— 

( 1 ) as is sold or otherwise disposed of to the occupier 
of any factory ; of 

(ii) as is used by the owner of such mine for any pur¬ 
pose in connection with the manufacture of cement, 
iron or steel, ferro-alloys, alloy steel, chemicals, 
sugar, paper fertilizers, refractories, Iron Ore pelleti¬ 
sation or such other article or goods or class of arti¬ 
cles or goods, as the Central Government may from 
time to time specify by notification in the official 
Gazette. 

to promote the welfare of the person* employed in Limestone 
and Dolomite Mines. The actual rate of levy presently is 
20 paisc per metric tonne. Tire proceeds of the cess arc being 
utilised mainly for the improvement of public health and sani¬ 
tation, provision of medical facilities subsidy on housing and 
nutrition programme etc. 

For administrative convenience, the nineteen States and 
Union Territories of Goa and Delhi, which have limestone 
and dolomite mines in the country, have been grouped into 
nine regions and each region is placed under the over all 
charge of the Welfare Commissioner. The Welfare Commis- 
•ioner of the aieas have been appointed as Welfare and Cess 
Commissioner for the enforcement of the Act and Rules 
framed there under. The allocation of the region is as 
under

3. A Central Advisory Committee and 6 State Advisory 
Committees, for the States of Bihar, Madhya Pradesh, Kar¬ 
nataka, Orissa, Rajasthan and Uttar Pradesh have been re¬ 
constituted under the Limestone and Dolomite Mines Labour 
Welfare Fund Act, 1972. The State Advisory Committees in 
respect of Maharashtra, Gujarat, Tamil Nadu and Andhra 
Pradesh are being reconstituted. The Committees meet perio¬ 
dically and advise the Government rcgaiding 'administration 
of the Act. 

4, The following welfare facilities, have been provided to 
the Limestone and Dolomite Mines workers: 

A. Health 

Thirteen Ayurvedic dispensaries, 20 allopathic dispensaries, 
one Malernity-cum-Child Welfaie Centre set up earlier by 
the Welfare Fund Organisation have continued to give medi¬ 
cal treatment to the limestone and dolomite .mines workers 
and their dependents. Four more dispensaries (2 in Rajasthan 
and 2 in Jabalpur) were sanctioned during 1985-86. Granls- 
in-aid of Rs. 21,305 were given to the two mine managements 
for maintaining their dispensaries. 62 beds have been reserved 
in TB hospitals 'for the treatment of workers suffering from 
TB. 784 workers tvere given treatment who were suffering 
from TB during the 1985-86. Benefits have been given in 
28 cases tinder Fatal and Serious Accident Benefits Scheme. 
The total expenditure during 1985-86 under the Health was 
Rs. 30,53,483,52. 

B. Education 

Under the Scheme for (he award of scholarships, scholar¬ 
ships arc granted 1o the sons and daughters of th.e regular 
limestone and dolomite mine worker* whose monthly income 
do not exceed Rs. 1600 p.m. The scheme envisages award 
of scholarship* for class V onwards for technical education. 




t*rpr II—nr* 3(il)l 


degree course, medical and engineering courses at the fates 
varying from Rs. 15 to Rs. 125 pm, per student. During 
1985-86, Rs, 7,71,384.50 was spent op,.education out\of which 
sum of Rs. 5,37,863 has been awaided as scholarship to 1910 
children of mines workers during 1985-86.' 

C. Recreation 

Iwo mobile Cinema Units m Jabalpur region, one in 
Bhubaneswar region, 4 in AllahabatLiegion and 3 in Banga¬ 
lore region continued to function during the year under re- 
poit, A Holiday Home was set up at Puri in Orissa for the 
Limestone and Dolomite Mine workers. Twenty-eight, 16 mm 
projectors with accessories have been provided to different 
mine managements, Grants-in-aid for re-imbuisement of films 
hire charges are being paid to the mine managements. Dur¬ 
ing the year under report sum of Rs. 10,13,814 was spent 
on recreational facilities. 

D. Water Supply 

Duriug the year under jeport sum of Rs. 3,65,566,89 out 
of Rs. 6,53,000 was sanfeioned/spent under the Water Sup¬ 
ply Scheme to the following manage menu: 

1, A subsidy of Rs. 20,565.00 was sanctioned for provid¬ 
ing water pipelines to the M/s. UP State Mineral Develop¬ 
ment Corporation Ltd. 

2, Subsidy of Rs. 3-09 lakhs was sanctioned for Water 
Treatment Plant to M/s. SAIL, Purnapani in Orissa. 

3, Subsidy of Rs. 36,000 was sanctioned to M/s. Raymond 
Cement Works Gopal Nagar in M.P. 

E. Housing 

Provision of Housing accommodation for limestone and 
dolomite workers is one of the main activities. 

Presently, there are three schemes in vogue, namely :— 

(1) Type I Housing Scheme 

(2) Type II Housing Scheme 

1,3) Build Your Own House Scheme 

(.lj Under Type 1 Housing scheme subsidy is payable at 
the rate of 75 per cent ,of the standard estimated cost or 
Rs. 7500, whichever is less. In addition, development charges 
are also payable at the rate of 50 per cent of Rs. 2,000 for 
ordinary areas and 75 per cent of Rs. 2,000 for black cotton 
or bweily soil areas or the actual cost, whichever is less. The 
development charges, will inter alia include external and in¬ 
ternal water supply, sanitation, electricity and approach 
roads. During 1985-86 financial year, 3995 houses were sanc¬ 
tioned under Type I Housing Scheme for Limestone and dolo¬ 
mite mine workers.

Workers Welfare Fund Act, 1976 (62 of 1976 ), the Cen¬ 
tral Government hereby publishes the folloviag repoit on 
the -activities financed under the said Act, during the year 
ending 31 st Mirch, 1986 — 

General 

The Beedi W'orhos Welfare fund has leen ccnstiltued 
under the Beedi Workers Welfare Cess Act, 1976 , and the 
Beedi Workers Welfare Fund Act, 1976 , foi financing of 
treasures to promote *hc welt arc of peisons engaged in beedi

MEDICAL CARF 

Organisation of an infrastructure f<8r rendering medical 
care has been given top priority'. Variou* types of medical 
facilities for Beedi Workers and their dependents are being 
provided free of cost by the Labour Welfare Oigamsarion. 
These include provision and maintenance of hospitals, dis¬ 
pensaries maternity ap.l child welfare centres, facilities fo>- 
treatment of TB etc. 

In addition to the existing medical Institutions 18 new dis- 
ensaries (one in Bangalore retion, 2. in Bhubaneswar regoin, 
in Calcutta legion, 5 in Nagpur region, 2 in Hyderabad 


tegton and 6, nj Jabalpur region,) v.epe - -anctioaed during 
the year Under report. The number of dispensaries sanction¬ 
ed eatlier stood at 116. The Welfare Organisation have 
been endeavouring !o provide adequate facilities for treat¬ 
ment of Beedi v.uiie/, sum ring from T.b. 84 beds have 
been icserved for the treatment of workers who are suffering 
from T.B. 32040 workers have availed of the medical facili¬ 
ties during the period. 10 woikcis were given financial assis¬ 
tance for the purchase of spectacles. 

An eye Examination Camp and Gyanocological camp 
were arranged in the Allahabad region for the welfare of 
the beedi workers.
